STAFF REPORT 
 
To:   Eastern Summit County Planning Commission   
From:   Jennifer Strader, Principal Planner 
Date of Meeting: November 21, 2024  
Type of Item: Rezone/Master Planned Development – Public Hearing and Possible 
Action 
Process:  Legislative  
File #:   21-260 
 
 
Recommendation: Staff has reviewed the application for compliance with all standards 
in the Eastern Summit County Development Code (Code) and found that it meets the minimum 
required for approval.  Staff recommends that the Eastern Summit County  Planning 
Commission (ESCPC) review the proposed Rezone/Master Planned Development (MPD), 
conduct a  public hearing , and forward a positive recommendation to the Summit County 
Council (SCC), pursuant to the findings of fact, conclusions of law, and conditions of approval 
found in this Staff Report. 
 
Project Description 
Project Name:   Blue Sky Ranch Rezone/Master Planned Development 
Applicant(s):   Michael Phillips 
Property Owner(s):  Phillips Venture 5 LLC 
Location:   Wanship, Utah 
Zone District:    AG-40 
Parcel Number and Size:  NS-157 (541.68 acres) and NS-158 (3.20 acres) 
Type of Process:  Legislative 
Final Land Use Authority: Summit County Council 
 
Proposal 
The applicant, Michael Phillips, is requesting to rezone Parcel NS-157, containing 551 acres, and 
Parcel NS-158, containing 3.20 acres, from AG-40 to AG-10.   
 
Concurrently, the applicant proposes a Conditional Use Permit (CUP)/MPD to expand the 
existing Blue Sky Ranch, which is classified in the Code as a “Guest Ranch or Lodge Intended to 
Attract Visitors/Patrons on a Daily Basis or Extended Stay” onto Parcels NS-157 (551 acres), NS-
154 (40 acres), NS-158 (3.20 acres), NS-224 (5.17 acres), DELGHRY-1-AM (6.00 acres), DELGHRY-
2-AM (33.72 acres), totaling 639.57 acres. 
 
6
2 
 
The applicant also proposes to subdivide the aforementioned parcels into 76 lots, processed 
under the bonus density provisions according to Appendix B of the Code. Seventy-two (72) lots 
are proposed for single family residential homes that could be put into the rental pool for Blue 
Sky Ranch. The residential lot sizes range from 0.50 acres to 5.0 acres. The remaining four (4) 
lots will be used for amenities that will be available for all Blue Sky guests and residents. 
 
Note: Please refer to the accompanying staff report, dated November 7, 2024, for the details 
related to the CUP/MPD and Subdivision/MPD. 
 
Vicinity Map for Rezone 
 
 
 
 
NS-157 
NS-158 
AG-80 AG-40 AG-10 
7
3 
 
Vicinity Map for Subdivision and CUP 
 
 
 
Background 
Blue Sky Ranch received a CUP in 2006 for a “Guest Ranch or Lodge Intended to Attract 
Visitors/Patrons on a Daily Basis or Extended Stay” on Parcels NS-86 (316.17 acres), BSL-1 
(707.63 acres), and BSL-2 (5 acres), totaling ~1,028 acres. The definition of a “Guest Ranch or 
Lodge” is:  
 
“A commercial use consisting of recreational activities that may include, but are not limited to, 
horseback riding, fishing, hunting, skiing and snowmobiling. The guest ranch may include 
overnight lodging, food service, meeting and conference facilities as well as other uses”. 
 
Proposed facilities on the property included: 
 
• a conference center 
• wellness center   
• fitness facility 
• pool 
• lodging, consisting of 171 guest rooms  
• 5 restaurants 
• a retail store 
• commercial arenas 
• commercial stables, agricultural buildings 
8
4 
 
• incidental structures such as maintenance and administration buildings  
• Other anticipated uses included traditional conferences, weddings and other special 
events, corporate training, food and beverage services, and recreational activities such 
as horseback riding and mountain bike riding.  
 
In 2016, the applicant applied for a Low Impact Permit to change the layout and uses, which 
resulted in a reduction of square footage. Section 11-4-7.G.1 of the Code establishes the 
process for a minor amendment to an approved CUP. It states: 
 
“A minor amendment is defined as an amendment that does not increase the square footage, 
density, or intensity of a previously approved conditional use permit. A minor amendment may 
be approved by the Community Development Director. No public hearing is required.”  
 
The Low Impact Permit was approved for approximately half the density. Current uses per the 
Low Impact Permit approval include: 
 
• Nineteen (19) Room Hotel/Restaurant 
• Twenty-two (22) Earth Suites (part of the lodging) 
• Five (5) Creek Houses (part of the lodging) 
• SPA facilities 
• Conference and Event Barn 
• Equestrian/Operations/Support Buildings 
• High West Distillery Facilities 
• Single Family Dwelling 
 
In 2024, the applicant requested to amend the CUP for what is referred to as the Crescent 
development, located north of the existing facilities. The CUP was approved and includes: 
 
• 16 lodging rooms 
• restaurant and bar 
• pool 
• spa 
 
Analysis and Findings 
Section 11-5-3 of the Development Code states that amendments to the Zoning Map shall not 
be granted until both the Planning Commission and County Council have reviewed the specific 
development proposal and determined compliance with the following standards:  
 
Standard 1: The amendment is generally consistent with the goals of the general plan. 
COMPLIES 
 
9
5 
 
Analysis: The Eastern Summit County General Plan emphasizes the need to balance 
economic growth and diversity with the preservation of Summit County’s agricultural 
heritage and natural resources.  The proposed rezone area is currently vacant and used 
for agriculture. Changing the zoning on the property would enable all allowed, 
conditional and low impact uses as stated in the Chapter 3 Use Table for the AG-10 
Zoning District, unless specifically limited by the MPD and Development Agreement.  
The proposed rezone is consistent with the goals and policies of the Eastern Summit 
County General Plan because it diversifies the economic base of the County.  
 
Standard 2: The amendment is compatible with adjacent land uses and will not be overly 
burdensome on the local community. COMPLIES 
 
Analysis: The site is accessed from Old Lincoln Highway. Blue Sky Ranch is located to the 
west and is zoned AG-80. Big Canyon Ranch, a Christian youth camp, is located to the 
north and is also zoned AG-80. There are a few vacant parcels located to the south and 
east. There are also lots within residential subdivisions located to the east which are 
zoned AG-10. Applicable service providers have reviewed the proposal and stated they 
have capacity to serve the proposed development, which is an expansion of Blue Sky 
Ranch. Staff finds the use is compatible with adjacent land uses and will not be overly 
burdensome on the local community.   
 
Standard 3: The specific development plan is in compliance with all applicable standards and 
criteria for approval as described in Chapter 4 of this title. COMPLIES  
 
Analysis: Chapter 4 refers to the development approval processes. A rezone must be 
accompanied by an MPD as well as the applicable development permit(s) for the 
proposed use(s), those being a CUP/MPD and Subdivision/MPD. Staff has evaluated the 
criteria for approval for the CUP/MPD and Subdivision/MPD in the accompanying staff 
report, dated November 7, 2024. 
 
Standard 4: The amendment does not adversely affect the public health, safety and general 
welfare. COMPLIES  
 
Analysis: Concurrent with this application, the applicant submitted a Subdivision/MPD 
and CUP/Master Planned Development application. The applications are being 
processed concurrently. Conditions of approval can be applied to ensure that the rezone 
does not adversely affect public health, safety, and general welfare. The associated 
development plans need to demonstrate compliance with the Development Evaluation 
Standards in Chapter 2 of the Code. 
 
Staff has conditioned the Subdivision/MPD and CUP/MPD so that if the Rezone/MPD is 
not approved by the County Council, the Subdivision/MPD and CUP/MPD are void.  
 
10
6 
 
Recommendation 
Staff recommends that the ESCPC review the proposed Rezone/MPD, conduct a public hearing, 
and vote to forward a positive recommendation to the Summit County Council, pursuant to the 
following findings of fact, conclusions of law, and condition of approval. 
 
Findings of Fact: 
1. The applicant, Michael Phillips, is requesting to rezone Parcel NS-157, containing 551 
acres, and Parcel NS-158, containing 3.20 acres, from AG-40 to AG-10.   
2. Parcels NS-157 and NS-158 are owned by Phillips Venture 5 LLC. 
3. Parcels NS-157 and NS-158 are located in Wanship, Utah. 
4. Concurrent with the request to rezone, the applicant proposes a Conditional Use Permit 
(CUP)/MPD to expand the existing Blue Sky Ranch, which is classified in the Code as a 
“Guest Ranch or Lodge Intended to Attract Visitors/Patrons on a Daily Basis or Extended 
Stay” onto Parcels NS-157 (551 acres), NS-154 (40 acres), NS-158 (3.20 acres), NS-224 
(5.17 acres), DELGHRY-1-AM (6.00 acres), DELGHRY-2-AM (33.72 acres), totaling 639.57. 
5. The applicant also proposes to subdivide the aforementioned parcels into 76 lots, 
processed under the bonus density provisions according to Appendix B of the Code. 
6. Seventy-two (72) lots are proposed for single family residential homes that could be put 
into the rental pool for Blue Sky Ranch. 
7. The residential lot sizes range from 0.50 acres to 5.0 acres.  
8. The remaining four (4) lots will be used for amenities that will be available for all Blue 
Sky guests and residents. 
9. Blue Sky Ranch received a CUP in 2006 for a “Guest Ranch or Lodge Intended to Attract 
Visitors/Patrons on a Daily Basis or Extended Stay” on Parcels NS-86 (316.17 acres), BSL-
1 (707.63 acres), and BSL-2 (5 acres), totaling ~1,028 acres. 
10. The definition of a “Guest Ranch or Lodge” is:  
“A commercial use consisting of recreational activities that may include, but are not 
limited to, horseback riding, fishing, hunting, skiing and snowmobiling. The guest ranch 
may include overnight lodging, food service, meeting and conference facilities as well as 
other uses”. 
11. Proposed facilities on the property included: 
a. a conference center 
b. wellness center   
c. fitness facility 
d. pool 
e. lodging, consisting of 171 guest rooms  
f. 5 restaurants 
g. a retail store 
h. commercial arenas 
i. commercial stables, agricultural buildings 
j. and incidental structures such as a maintenance and administration buildings.  
11
7 
 
k. Other anticipated uses included traditional conferences, weddings and other 
special events, corporate training, food and beverage services, and recreational 
activities such as horseback riding and mountain bike riding. 
12. In 2016, the applicant applied for a Low Impact Permit to change the layout and uses, 
which resulted in a reduction of square footage. Section 11-4-7.G.1 of the Code 
establishes the process for a minor amendment to an approved CUP. 
13. The Low Impact Permit was approved for approximately half the density. Current uses 
per the Low Impact Permit approval include: 
a. Nineteen (19) Room Hotel/Restaurant 
b. Twenty-two (22) Earth Suites (part of the lodging) 
c. Five (5) Creek Houses (part of the lodging) 
d. SPA facilities 
e. Conference and Event Barn 
f. Equestrian/Operations/Support Buildings 
g. High West Distillery Facilities 
h. Single Family Dwelling 
14. In 2024, the applicant requested to amend the CUP for what is referred to as the 
Crescent development, located north of the existing facilities. The CUP was approved 
and includes: 
a. 16 lodging rooms 
b. restaurant and bar 
c. pool 
d. spa 
15. The proposed rezone area is currently vacant and used for agriculture.  
16. Changing the zoning on the property would enable all allowed, conditional and low 
impact uses as stated in the Chapter 3 Use Table for the AG-10 Zoning District, unless 
specifically limited by the MPD and Development Agreement.  
17. The site is accessed from Old Lincoln Highway.  
18. Blue Sky Ranch is located to the west and is zoned AG-80.  
19. Big Canyon Ranch, a Christian youth camp, is located to the north and is also zoned AG-
80.  
20. There are a few vacant parcels located to the south and east. There are also lots within 
residential subdivisions located to the east which are zoned AG-10. 
21. Applicable service providers have reviewed the proposal and stated they have capacity 
to serve the proposed development, which is an expansion of Blue Sky Ranch. 
A rezone must be accompanied by an MPD as well as the applicable development 
permit(s) for the proposed use(s), those being a CUP/MPD and Subdivision/MPD.  
22. Staff has evaluated the criteria for approval for the CUP/MPD and Subdivision/MPD in 
the accompanying staff report, dated November 7, 2024. 
 
 
 
 
12
8 
 
Conclusions of Law:  
1. The amendment is generally consistent with the goals of the general plan.  
2. The amendment is compatible with adjacent land uses and will not be overly 
burdensome on the local community.  
3. The specific development plans are in compliance with all applicable standards and 
criteria for approval as described in Chapter 4 of this title. 
4. The amendment does not adversely affect the public health, safety and general welfare. 
 
Conditions of Approval:  
1. Prior to the rezone taking effect, a Development Agreement shall be signed by the Chair 
of the County Council and shall include, at a minimum, the following: 
a. A legal description of the land. 
b. All conditions of approval, relevant zoning, and development code parameters, 
including all findings of fact and conclusions of law, specifying any exceptions, 
including, but not limited to those outlining more or less restrictive height or 
setbacks. 
c. An express reservation of the future legislative power and zoning authority of 
the County. 
d. A copy of the approved site plan, architectural plans, landscaping plans, grading 
plan, trails and open space plans, and other plans, which are part of the 
approval. 
e. A description of all developer exactions or agreed upon public dedications. 
f. The developer’s agreement to pay all specified impact fees. 
g. The form of ownership anticipated for the project. 
h. A specific project phasing plan. 
 
Public Notice, Meetings and Comments  
This item was publicly noticed as a public hearing with possible action by the ESCPC. Notice of 
this public hearing was published in the November 6, 2024 issue of The Summit County News. 
Postcard courtesy notices were also mailed to property owners within 1,000 feet of the subject 
parcel prior to both public hearings. As of the date of this report, no public comment has been 
received.

AGENDA ITEM NO. 4
Staff Report
 
 
DATE:
November 21, 2024
SUBMITTED BY:
Jennifer Strader, Community Development
ITEM TYPE:
Action
AGENDA SECTION:
6:00 P.M. Regular Session
SUBJECT:
Public hearing, possible action regarding a Conditional Use Permit,
Subdivision, and Master Planned Development. The applicant
requests to expand the existing Blue Sky Ranch Conditional Use
Permit for 
a "Guest Ranch or Lodge Intended to Attract
Visitors/Patrons on a Daily Basis or Extended Stay" onto Parcels 
NS-
157 (551 acres), NS-154 (40 acres), NS-158 (3.20 acres), NS-224 (5.17
acres), DELGHRY-1-AM (6.00 acres), DELGHRY-2-AM (33.72 acres),
totaling 639.57 acres, located in Wanship, Utah. 
The applicant also
proposes to subdivide the aforementioned parcels into 76 lots,
processed under the bonus density provisions according to Appendix
B of the Code. Applicant: Michael Phillips. Owner: Phillips Venture
LLC. Administrative review. Project #s 21-261 and 24-138. Jennifer
Strader, 
Principal Planner.

File #s:   21-261 (Subdivision/MPD) and 24-138 (Conditional Use Permit/MPD) 
 
 
I. Recommendation: Staff has reviewed the applications for compliance with all 
standards in the Eastern Summit County Development Code (Code) and found that they m eet 
the minimum required for approval.  Staff recommends that the Eastern Summit County 
Planning Commission (ESCPC) review the proposed Conditional Use Permit/Master Planned 
Development (CUP/MPD) and Subdivision/MPD (Exhibits A1-A79), conduct a  public hearing 
and approve the  applications, pursuant t o the findings of fact, conclusions of law, and 
conditions of approval found in this Staff Report. 
 
II. Project Description 
Project Name:   The Farms at Blue Sky CUP/MPD and Subdivision/MPD 
Applicant(s):   Michael Phillips  
Property Owner(s):  Phillips Venture 5 LLC  
Location:   Wanship, Utah  
Zone District: AG-10 (assuming the accompanying rezone application, file #21-
260, is approved) 
Parcel Number and Size: Parcels NS-157 (551 acres), NS-154 (40 acres), NS-158 (3.20 
acres), NS-224 (5.17 acres), DELGHRY-1-AM (6.00 acres), 
DELGHRY-2-AM (33.72 acres) 
Type of Process:  Administrative 
Final Land Use Authority: Planning Commission 
 
III. Proposal 
The applicant, Michael Phillips, proposes a CUP/MPD to expand the existing Blue Sky Ranch, 
which is classified in the Code as a “Guest Ranch or Lodge Intended to Attract Visitors/Patrons 
on a Daily Basis or Extended Stay” onto Parcels NS-157 (551 acres), NS-154 (40 acres), NS-158 
(3.20 acres), NS-224 (5.17 acres), DELGHRY-1-AM (6.00 acres), DELGHRY-2-AM (33.72 acres), 
totaling 639.57 acres.  
94
2 
 
The definition of a “Guest Ranch or Lodge” is:  
 
“A commercial use consisting of recreational activities that may include, but are not limited to, 
horseback riding, fishing, hunting, skiing and snowmobiling. The guest ranch may include 
overnight lodging, food service, meeting and conference facilities as well as other uses”. 
 
The applicant also proposes to subdivide the aforementioned parcels into 76 lots, processed 
under the bonus density provisions according to Appendix B of the Code. Seventy-two (72) lots 
are proposed for single family residential homes that could be put into the rental pool for Blue 
Sky Ranch. The residential lot sizes range from 0.50 acres to 5.0 acres. The remaining four (4) 
lots will be used for amenities that will be available for all Blue Sky guests and residents. The 
amenities include: 
 
• Art and Music Studio 
• Fitness Center 
• Learning Green House 
• Meditation Garden 
• Ponds 
• Community Center 
• Gourmet Grocery 
• Recreational Open Space 
• Equestrian Riding (located within an existing building) 
• Wedding Preparation Facility (located within an existing building 
• Outdoor Adventure Staging 
• Administrative Offices (located within an existing building) 
 
IV. Vicinity Map

V. Background 
Blue Sky Ranch The Farms at Blue Sky 
10.18.06: CUP for a “Guest Ranch or Lodge Intended to 
Attract Visitors/Patrons on a Daily Basis or Extended Stay” 
on Parcels NS-82 (712.63 acres) and NS-86 (316.17 acres), 
totaling ~1,028 acres, was approved by the ESCPC. Proposed 
facilities on the property included: 
 
• a conference center 
• wellness center 
• fitness facility 
• pool 
• lodging, consisting of 171 guest rooms  
• 5 restaurants 
• a retail store 
• commercial arenas 
• commercial stables, agricultural buildings 
• incidental structures such as maintenance and 
administration buildings  
• Other anticipated uses included traditional 
conferences, weddings and other special events, 
corporate training, food and beverage services, 
and recreational activities such as horseback riding 
and mountain bike riding.  
 
10.17.16: Low Impact Permit to change the layout and uses 
for Blue Sky Ranch, which resulted in a reduction of square 
footage, was approved by the Community Development 
Director. The Low Impact Permit was approved for 
approximately half the density. Current uses per the Low 
Impact Permit approval include: 
 
• Nineteen (19) Room Hotel/Restaurant 
• Twenty-two (22) Earth Suites (part of the lodging) 
• Five (5) Creek Houses (part of the lodging) 
• SPA facilities 
• Conference and Event Barn 
• Equestrian/Operations/Support Buildings 
• High West Distillery Facilities 
• Single Family Dwelling 
 
3.15.22: Blue Sky Lodge Subdivision Plat, consisting of 2 lots, 
was recorded on Parcel NS-82 located at Blue Sky Ranch. The 
final land use authority was the Community Development 
Director. 
 
2.1.24: CUP approved for sixteen (16) additional lodging 
rooms, a restaurant, and a spa and yoga facility on Parcel 
NS-82, located at Blue Sky Ranch, named The Crescent. 
 
6.10.24: Applicant applied for a two (2) lot subdivision on 
Parcel NS-86, located at Blue Sky Ranch. The subdivision was 
approved by the Community Development Director, but has 
yet to be recorded. 
10.14.20: Applicant applied for a sketch plan to get 
preliminary feedback for a proposed rezone for Parcel NS-
157 from AG-40 to AG-10. This parcel is adjacent to the 
existing Blue Sky Ranch facilities. The Sketch Plan also 
included a request to subdivide Parcel NS-157 into sixty-four 
(64) residential lots using the bonus density provision of the 
Code. The subdivision included outbuildings, a clubhouse, 
pool, and farming area. The project is called The Farms at 
Blue Sky. 
 
12.17.20: ESCPC held a work session for The Farms at Blue 
Sky and provided initial comments related to sewer, the 
safety of Old Lincoln Highway, employee housing, steep 
slopes, and the potential of homes being located on 
ridgelines. 
 
10.21.21: ESCPC held another work to discuss the rezone 
and subdivision for The Farms at Blue Sky, but the proposed 
amenities were removed from the application. Similar 
concerns were raised as those discussed at the 12.17.20 
work session.  
 
12.30.21: Applicant submitted a formal Rezone/MPD and 
Subdivision/MPD application for The Farms at Blue Sky on 
Parcel NS-157. 
 
6.6.22: Applicant applied for a Low Impact Permit for a 
portion of the roads that cross slopes greater than 30% in 
The Farms at Blue Sky development. The final land use 
authority for a low impact permit is the Community 
Development Director. This application is pending until final 
action is taken on the Rezone, CUP, Subdivision, and MPD.  
 
Between 2022 and 2024, the applicant and staff had 
numerous discussions regarding the applicant’s intent with 
The Farms at Blue Sky. The Community Development 
Director determined that the request is an expansion of the 
existing Blue Sky Ranch.

VI. Analysis and Findings for the Subdivision 
An applicant is eligible to apply for a bonus density subdivision if they provide a certain amount 
of deed restricted open space for public or private agricultural, scenic, or recreational purposes. 
Deed restricted open space does not include open areas in private individual residential lots, 
public roads, private road, parking spaces and drive aisles in parking lots, land covered by 
structures not designated for active civic recreational use, and outdoor storage areas. 
 
Assuming the associated rezone application is approved, resulting in Parcels NS-157 and NS-158 
being zoned AG-10 zone, the base density, based on 639.57 acres, is 63 lots. The applicant is 
requesting a total of 76 lots, which requires a minimum of 312.30 acres of open space. 
Additionally, no more than 50% of the required deed restricted open space can consist of 
undevelopable lands, such as steep slopes. The applicant will deed restrict 312.30 acres of open 
space and a maximum of 156 acres of that open space will have less than a 30% slope.   
 
Before a subdivision can be approved, it must conform to the following criteria: 
 
Standard 1: All of the land required for the density to create the lots within the subdivision shall 
be contained within the boundaries of the subdivision plat. COMPLIES 
 
Analysis: Parcels NS-157 (551 acres), NS-154 (40 acres), NS-158 (3.20 acres), NS-224 
(5.17 acres), DELGHRY-1-AM (6.00 acres), DELGHRY-2-AM (33.72 acres), totaling 639.57, 
are included in the boundaries of the subdivision. The applicant is not proposing 
remnant parcels. 
 
Standard 2: Each proposed lot shall have legal access through a recorded right-of-way or 
easement. The applicant shall demonstrate that adequate access to the property from a public 
road may be granted by the State or County, whichever is applicable. COMPLIES 
 
Analysis: Access is via Old Lincoln Highway, which is a county road. The Summit County 
Engineering Department reviewed the proposal and granted access.  
 
Standard 3: Compliance with the development evaluation standards provided in chapter 2 of 
the code. COMPLIES

Natural Resources   
Development shall minimize the 
highly visible placement of homes on 
hillsides. It should be sensitively 
sited. 
 
 
The proposed subdivision is setback a half mile on the east 
Wanship bench where the homes will be minimally visible 
from below.  
 
The subdivision plat contains a note, part of which states: 
“…home designs within the subdivision require exterior colors 
that blend with the natural surroundings”, which will further 
reduce visibility. 
COMPLIES 
Development is discouraged in a 100-
year floodplain. 
There are no floodplains on the property per the Summit 
County Engineering Department review. 
COMPLIES 
No development is permitted in 
wetlands. 
There are no wetlands on the property, as evidenced by the 
USGS wetland mapper.   
COMPLIES 
No development is permitted on 
slopes in excess of 30%. 
A slope analysis submitted by the applicant indicates the 
presence of slopes greater than 30%. Section 11-2-4-G of the 
Code states, “In the event access to a suitable development 
area…requires the crossing of an area of thirty percent (30%) 
slope, the Community Development Director may approve 
development as a low impact permit…” The applicant applied 
for a Low Impact Permit for roads that cross 30% slopes. That 
permit is being reviewed concurrently with these 
applications.  Additionally, during the building permit review 
for the residences, Staff will ensure no development will 
occur on areas of the lot(s) that contain 30% slopes or 
greater. 
COMPLIES  
(as conditioned) 
Care shall be taken to ensure that 
development shall not significantly 
affect in a negative fashion wildlife 
birthing areas, critical winter range 
areas and migration corridors. 
The applicant had a third party, Frontier Corporation Environmental 
Consultants prepare a Big Game Wildlife Habitat Assessment (Exhibits 
I1-I8). The study found the property contains “crucial summer, fall 
and winter habitat for elk, substantial winter habitat for mule deer, 
but no crucial or substantial habitat for moose.” The Utah Division of 
Wildlife Services representative stated her comment would be similar 
to those provided during her review of the existing Blue Sky Ranch 
(Exhibits J1-J3).

Infrastructure/Facilities/ Services   
No development shall cause the traffic

below the design capacity of the

The applicant had a traffic study prepared by Hales

County Engineer reviewed the traffic study and stated, “The 
amount of traffic in this area isn’t enough to warrant 
anything.” However, the applicant is proposing the following 
improvements on Old Lincoln Highway: 
 
• Yellow striping down the center to identify passing 
zones and associated signs. 
• White shoulder stripes to narrow the lanes. 
• Provide and install 35 PH signage as traffic exits Blue 
Sky Ranch. 
• Provide and install 35 MPH signage approximately 1 
mile north and south of the entrance to Blue Sky 
Ranch. 
• Speed limit pavement markings at both the east and 
west end of the 25 MPH zone. 
COMPLIES 
Development that presents an 
unusual fire hazard which is beyond 
the capability of the fire district will 
not be approved. 
The chief of the North Summit Fire District reviewed the 
applications and stated he has no concerns with the proposal. 
He indicated he has had many meetings with Blue Sky who 
have installed their own water system and have fire hydrants 
every 500’. They also have plans to have more than 30’ of 
clear space around all buildings.  
COMPLIES 
Development in remote locations that 
could affect emergency services will 
not be approved. 
The chief of the North Summit Fire District reviewed the

COMPLIES

Standard 4: Compliance with the infrastructure standards in chapter 6 of the code. COMPLIES 
(as conditioned) 
 
Analysis: The infrastructure standards refer to fire and road requirements. Fire 
standards were addressed in the previous table.  
 
The roads within the subdivision are private. The main access roads have 22’ of 
pavement with 2’ gravel shoulders on each side of the road, within a 44’ right of way. 
Other roads have 20’ of pavement with a shoulder containing 1’ of paved surface and 1’ 
of gravel, within a 40’ right of way. 
 
The Summit County Engineering Department reviewed the infrastructure construction 
drawings and requested the applicant provide a few more details on the plan. These 
plans are required to be approved prior to building permit issuance.  
 
Standard 5: The minimum lot size for new lots created through this process will meet the 
minimum lot size requirements for the applicable zone. COMPLIES 
 
Analysis: Lots that are approved through a subdivision and MPD and provide culinary 
water and sewer service may be permitted lot sizes of ½ acre. 
 
The applicant requests a minimum lot size of ½ acre for 46 lots clustered in the center of 
the property. They will be provided with culinary water and sewer. Per the applicant, 
“The clustering of residences will allow for the preservation of more than double the 
amount of agricultural land and open space”.

Summit County Water Conservancy Special Service District, depending on the subdivision’s 
type. COMPLIES 
 
Analysis: Wastewater will be provided through an on-site sewage treatment plant that

letter from the Utah State Department of Environmental Quality for the treatment 
plant. 
 
The Summit County Health Department reviewed the details of the onsite sewage 
treatment plant and confirmed approval from the Utah State Department of 
Environmental Quality. 
 
The Eastern Summit County Water Conservancy Special Service District does not review 
or recommend approval of the specific type of treatment plant proposed by the 
applicant.

VIII. Analysis and Findings for the Master Planned Development 
Section 11-4-12 of the Code outlines the following requirements for a Master Planned 
Development.  
 
MPD Requirement Analysis Finding 
 
Density  
The type of development and number 
of units created shall be determined by 
a site suitability analysis. 
 
In the AG-10 zone district, a “Guest Ranch or Lodge Intended to 
Attract Visitors/Patrons on a Daily Basis or Extended Stay” 
requires a CUP. The layout of the facility must comply with the 
setbacks, height, parking, and all other applicable standards of 
the Code, as further evaluated in this report. 
 
COMPLIES 
 
 
Density Bonus 
A density bonus may be permitted in 
accordance with appendix B of the 
Code. 
 
 
The applicant is requesting a density bonus for the 
Subdivision/MPD. The base density for the entire acreage is 63 
lots. Because the applicant is proposing 76 lots, the required 
amount of open space is 312.30 acres. The applicant proposes 
to deed restrict 312.30 acres of open space. 
 
COMPLIES 
 
Setbacks 
The minimum setback around the 
exterior boundary shall match those 
of the more restrictive abutting zone 
district. In some cases, that setback 
may be increased to create an 
adequate buffer to adjacent uses.  
 
 
 
The applicant proposes a setback of at least 50’ around the 
property’s exterior perimeter which is larger than the required 
12’ setback for the adjacent zone district.

in lot size improves site design,

Master planned developments shall

disturbance of the lot will be required.

consideration. Development along 
the project perimeter shall 
adequately mitigate any potentially 
adverse effects, including, but not 
limited to flooding, erosion, 
subsidence, sloping of the soil or 
other dangers and nuisances. 
 
  
The development has been sited so that are no residences or 
other structures within 300’ of any other projects.  
 
The property to the north is undeveloped; along the east 
property line there are two residences located in the Fox Run 
Hollow Subdivision. They are located ~350’ from the east 
property line of the project. VP Laboratories is located to the 
south of the property, ~400’ away from the south property 
line. Blue Sky Ranch is located to the west.

VIIII. Recommendation 
Staff recommends that the ESCPC review the proposed CUP/MPD and Subdivision/MPD, 
conduct a public hearing, and approve the applications, pursuant to the following findings of 
fact, conclusions of law, and conditions of approval. 
 
Findings of Fact 
1. On 10.18.06 a CUP for a “Guest Ranch or Lodge Intended to Attract Visitors/Patrons on 
a Daily Basis or Extended Stay” on Parcels NS-82 (712.63 acres) and NS-86 (316.17 
acres), totaling ~1,028 acres was approved by the ESCPC, named Blue Sky Ranch. 
2. Proposed facilities on the property included: 
o a conference center 
o wellness center 
o fitness facility 
o pool 
o lodging, consisting of 171 guest rooms  
o 5 restaurants 
o a retail store 
o commercial arenas 
o commercial stables, agricultural buildings 
o incidental structures such as 
o maintenance and administration buildings  
3. Other anticipated uses included traditional conferences, weddings and other special 
events, corporate training, food and beverage services, and recreational activities such 
as horseback riding and mountain bike riding.  
4. On 10.17.16 a Low Impact Permit to change the layout and uses for Blue Sky Ranch, 
which resulted in a reduction of square footage, was approved by the Community 
Development Director. The Low Impact Permit was approved for approximately half the 
density. 
5. Current uses per the Low Impact Permit approval include: 
o Nineteen (19) Room Hotel/Restaurant 
o Twenty-two (22) Earth Suites (part of the lodging) 
105
13 
 
o Five (5) Creek Houses (part of the lodging) 
o SPA facilities 
o Conference and Event Barn 
o Equestrian/Operations/Support Buildings 
o High West Distillery Facilities 
o Single Family Dwelling 
6. On 3.15.22, Blue Sky Lodge Subdivision Plat was recorded for a two (2) lot subdivision on 
Parcel NS-82 located at Blue Sky Ranch. The final land use authority was the Community 
Development Director. 
7. On 2.1.24 a CUP approved for sixteen (16) additional lodging rooms, a restaurant, and a 
spa and yoga facility on Parcel NS-82 located at Blue Sky Ranch, named The Crescent. 
8. On 6.10.24 the Applicant applied for a two (2) lot subdivision on Parce NS-86, located at 
Blue Sky Ranch. The subdivision was approved by the Community Development 
Director, but has yet to be recorded. 
9. On 10.14.20 the Applicant applied for a Sketch Plan to get preliminary feedback for a 
proposed rezone for Parcel NS-157 from AG-40 to AG-10.  
10. Parcel NS-157 is adjacent to the existing Blue Sky Ranch facilities.  
11. The Sketch Plan also included a request to subdivide Parcel NS-157 into sixty-four (64) 
residential lots using the bonus density provision of the Code. The subdivision included 
outbuildings, a clubhouse, pool, and farming area.  
12. The project is called The Farms at Blue Sky. 
13. On 12.17.20 the ESCPC held a work session for The Farms at Blue Sky and provided 
initial comments related to sewer, the safety of Old Lincoln Highway, employee housing, 
steep slopes, and the potential of homes being located on ridgelines. 
14. On 10.21.21 the ESCPC held another work to discuss the rezone and subdivision for The 
Farms at Blue Sky, but the proposed amenities were removed from the application. 
Similar concerns were raised as those discussed at the 12.17.20 work session.  
15. On 12.30.21 the Applicant submitted a formal Rezone/MPD and Subdivision/MPD 
application for The Farms at Blue Sky on Parcel NS-157. 
16. On 6.6.22 the Applicant applied for a Low Impact Permit for a portion of the roads that 
cross slopes greater than 30% in The Farms at Blue Sky development. The final land use 
authority for a Low Impact Permit is the Community Development Director.  
17. The Low Impact Permit application is pending until final action is taken on the Rezone, 
CUP, Subdivision, and MPD. 
18. Between 2022 and 2024, the applicant and staff had numerous discussions regarding 
the applicant’s intent with The Farms at Blue Sky. The Community Development Director 
determined that The Farms at Blue Sky is an expansion of the existing Blue Sky Ranch. 
19. On 8.26.24 the Applicant submitted a formal CUP/MPD for The Farms at Blue Sky on 
Parcels NS-157 (551 acres), NS-154 (40 acres), NS-158 (3.20 acres), NS-224 (5.17 acres), 
DELGHRY-1-AM (6.00 acres), DELGHRY-2-AM (33.72 acres), totaling 639.57 acres.  
20. The previously submitted Rezone/MPD and Subdivision/MPD applications will also apply 
to the aforementioned parcels, totaling 639.57 acres. 
21. An applicant is eligible to apply for a bonus density subdivision if they provide a certain 
amount of deed restricted open space for public or private agricultural, scenic, or 
recreational purposes.  
106
14 
 
22. Deed restricted open space does not include open areas in private individual residential 
lots, public roads, private road, parking spaces and drive aisles in parking lots, land 
covered by structures not designated for active civic recreational use, and outdoor 
storage areas. 
23. Assuming the associated rezone application is approved, resulting in Parcels NS-157 and 
NS-158 being zoned AG-10 zone, the base density, based on 639.57 acres, is 63 lots.  
24. The applicant is requesting a total of 76 lots, which requires a minimum of 312.30 acres 
of open space. Additionally, no more than 50% of the required deed restricted open 
space can consist of undevelopable lands, such as steep slopes.  
25. The applicant will deed restrict 312.30 acres of open space and a maximum of 156 acres 
of that open space will have less than a 30% slope.   
26. Parcels NS-157 (551 acres), NS-154 (40 acres), NS-158 (3.20 acres), NS-224 (5.17 acres), 
DELGHRY-1-AM (6.00 acres), DELGHRY-2-AM (33.72 acres), totaling 639.57, are included 
in the boundaries of the subdivision.  
27. The applicant is not proposing remnant parcels. 
28. Access is via Old Lincoln Highway, which is a county road.  
29. The Summit County Engineering Department reviewed the proposal and granted access.  
30. The following note is on the proposed plat: 
 
31.  A large portion of the property will continue to be used for agricultural purposes. 
32. The subdivision plat contains a note, part of which states: “…wildlife friendly fencing 
required…” 
33. The applicant submitted the following documents indicating compliance with the water 
requirements: 
o Water use summary 
o Exchange Application from the Utah State Division of Water Rights 
o Email from the Utah Division of Drinking Water re: water system meeting all 
standards with no violations 
o Letter from Loughlin Water Associates regarding well capacity and water quality 
o Letter from Loughlin Water Associates regarding radiological chemical testing 
o Email from the Summit County Health Department confirming radiological 
chemical testing 
34. Wastewater will be provided through an on-site sewage treatment plant that is owned 
and maintained by the applicant.  
35. Staff received an “Approval in Concept” letter from the Utah State Department of 
Environmental Quality for the wastewater system. 
36. The proposed subdivision is setback a half mile on the east Wanship bench.  
37. The subdivision plat contains a note, part of which states: “…home designs within the 
subdivision require exterior colors that blend with the natural surroundings”, which will 
further reduce visibility. 
107
15 
 
38. There are no floodplains on the property per the Summit County Engineering 
Department review. 
39. There are no wetlands on the property, as evidenced by the USGS wetland mapper.   
40. A slope analysis submitted by the applicant indicates the presence of slopes greater 
than 30%.  
41. Section 11-2-4-G of the Code states, “In the event access to a suitable development 
area…requires the crossing of an area of thirty percent (30%) slope, the Community 
Development Director may approve development as a low impact permit…”  
42. The applicant applied for a Low Impact Permit for roads that cross 30% slopes. That 
permit is being reviewed concurrently with these applications.   
43. During the building permit review for the residences, Staff will ensure no development 
will occur on areas of the lot(s) that contain 30% slopes or greater. 
44. The applicant had a third party, Frontier Corporation Environmental Consultants, 
prepare a Big Game Wildlife Habitat Assessment.  
45. The study found the property contains “crucial summer, fall and winter habitat for elk, 
substantial winter habitat for mule deer, but no crucial or substantial habitat for 
moose.”  
46. The applicant performed a ridgeline analysis per Section 11-2-4 (I) of the Code. At the 
time of this report, they found there may be a 2 or 3 lots that may ridgeline. Staff 
proposes a condition of approval that states: “Prior to building permit issuance for any 
structure that has the potential to ridgeline in a manner that causes any portion of the 
structure to extend into the skyline as viewed from a public road, Staff shall ensure the 
structure complies with section 11-2-4-I of the Code, as amended”.  
47. There are no streams located on the subject property.  
48. The Summit County Engineering Department reviewed the infrastructure construction 
drawings and requested the applicant provide a few more details on the plan. These 
plans are required to be approved prior to building permit issuance.  
49. Staff finds no evidence that the development will contribute to the degradation of air 
quality in the County. 
50. The applicant had a traffic study prepared by Hales Engineering which concluded no 
mitigation measures are required on Old Lincoln Highway.  
51. The Summit County Engineer reviewed the traffic study and stated, “The amount of 
traffic in this area isn’t enough to warrant anything.”  
52. The applicant is proposing the following improvements on Old Lincoln Highway: 
o Yellow striping down the center to identify passing zones and associated signs. 
o White shoulder stripes to narrow the lanes. 
o Provide and install 35 PH signage as traffic exits Blue Sky Ranch. 
o Provide and install 35 MPH signage approximately 1 mile north and south of the 
entrance to Blue Sky Ranch. 
o Speed limit pavement markings at both the east and west end of the 25 MPH 
zone. 
53. The chief of the North Summit Fire District reviewed the applications and stated he has 
no concerns with the proposal. He indicated he has had many meetings with Blue Sky 
who have installed their own water system and have fire hydrants every 500’. They also 
have plans to have more than 30’ of clear space around all buildings. 
108
16 
 
54. The development will be gated and staffed with a 24-hour guard. All emergency services 
will be provided with access. 
55. The roads within the subdivision are private. The main access roads have 22’ of 
pavement with 2’ gravel shoulders on each side of the road, within a 44’ right of way. 
Other roads have 20’ of pavement with a shoulder containing 1’ of paved surface and 1’ 
of gravel, within a 40’ right of way. 
56. Lots that are approved through a subdivision and MPD and provide culinary water and 
sewer service may be permitted lot sizes of ½ acre. 
57. The applicant requests a minimum lot size of ½ acre for 46 lots clustered in the center of 
the property. They will be provided with culinary water and sewer.  
58. The Eastern Summit County Water Conservancy Special Service District does not review 
or recommend approval of the specific type of treatment plant proposed by the 
applicant. 
59. Property taxes for the applicable property have been paid, per the Summit County 
Assessor’s Office. 
60. The amenity buildings are located the following distances from the property lines: 
Closest bldg. to roads (front) = 59’ 
Closest bldg. to east property line = 107.5’ 
Closest bldg. to northwest property line = 143.1’ 
61. Setbacks for residences will be reviewed and approved during building permit review. 
62. The Maximum height of the buildings are as follows: 
Larder = 31’6” 
Greenhouse = 30’9” 
Fitness = 29’9” 
Community Center = 31’4” 
Art Studio = 22’4” 
Music Studio = 28’4” 
63. Height for residences will be reviewed and approved during building permit review. 
64. The proposed development is an expansion of Blue Sky Ranch, located to the west. Big 
Canyon Ranch, a Christian youth camp, is located to the north. There are a few vacant 
parcels located to the south and east. There are also lots within residential subdivisions 
located to the east.  
65. County records indicate the properties are owned by Phillips Venture 5 LLC, of which 
Michael Phillips is the authorized representative. 
66. In the AG-10 zone district, a “Guest Ranch or Lodge Intended to Attract Visitors/Patrons 
on a Daily Basis or Extended Stay” requires a CUP. The layout of the facility must comply 
with the setbacks, height, parking, and all other applicable standards of the Code. 
67. The applicant proposes a setback of at least 50’ around the property’s exterior 
perimeter which is larger than the required 12’ setback for the adjacent zone district. 
68. The subdivision plat contains a note that all single family dwellings will contain 2 parking 
spaces. 
69. Based on the square footage of the commercial buildings, 32 parking spaces are 
required.  
70. The applicant proposes 45 parking spaces. 
109
17 
 
71. The development has been sited so that are no residences or other structures within 
300’ of any other projects. 
72. The property to the north is undeveloped; along the east property line there are two 
residences located in the Fox Run Hollow Subdivision. They are located ~350’ from the 
east property line of the project. VP Laboratories is located to the south of the property, 
~400’ away from the south property line. Blue Sky Ranch is located to the west. 
73. Electricity will be provided by Rocky Mountain Power; Dominion Energy will provide 
natural gas; All West Communications will provide phone, t.v. and internet. 
74. The subdivision plat contains the following note, part of which states: “…home designs 
within the subdivision require…passive solar features that take advantage of the 
building’s site, climate and materials to minimize energy use. Every lot in this subdivision 
shall include opportunities for passive solar features.” 
75. Bike lanes and sidewalks are not included in the road design. 
76. The subdivision plat contains a note, part of which states: “…Exterior lighting will be 
shielded to minimize glare and illumination of adjacent property.” 
 
Conclusions of Law 
1. All of the land required for the density to create the lots within the subdivision is 
contained within the boundaries of the subdivision plat. 
2. Each lot has legal access through a recorded right of way.  
3. The proposed subdivision complies with the development evaluation standards 
contained in chapter 2 of the Code. 
4. The proposed subdivision complies with the infrastructure standards contained in 
chapter 6 of the Code. 
5. The minimum lot size for new lots created through this process meets the minimum lot 
size requirements for the applicable zone. 
6. Property taxes for the property have been paid. 
7. The subdivision complies with all applicable County regulations. 
8. The proposed use, as conditioned, is appropriate in the particular location, taking into 
account the nature of the use, its relationship to surrounding uses, and its impact on the 
natural environment. 
9. The proposed use, as conditioned, complies with the development evaluation standards 
in chapter 2 of the Code. 
10. The applicant is the landowner. 
11. The MPD is designed to fit well into the natural terrain, minimize excessive site grading, 
and protect and preserve the surrounding area. 
12. The MPD makes suitable provisions for the protection, preservation, and enhancement 
of watercourses, drainage areas, wooded areas, rough terrain, and similar features. 
13. The MPD takes adjacent land uses into consideration and mitigates potential impacts, 
including, but not limited to flooding, erosion, subsidence, sloping of the soil, or other 
dangers and nuisances. 
14. The MPD has direct vehicular access from a public road and meets all requirements of 
the County Engineer and Fire Marshal.  
15. The MPD has a secondary point of access/emergency access.  
110
18 
 
16. Existing or proposed utility and public services are adequate to support the MPD at 
normal service levels and are designed in a manner to avoid adverse impacts on 
adjacent land uses, public services, and utility resources. 
17. The proposed structures within the MPD are located on reasonably developable 
portions of the site. The open areas within the MPD are designed so that existing 
vegetation can be maintained to the greatest degree possible. 
18. The MPD includes adequate internal vehicular circulation. 
19. The MPD includes areas for snow removal and snow storage.  
20. All exterior lighting within the MPD will be down directed and fully shielded. 
21. The MPD, as conditioned, complies with the requirements of the Code. 
22. The MPD, as conditioned, is consistent with the General Plan. 
23. The MPD has been publicly noticed and a public hearing held in accordance with the 
Code. 
 
Conditions of Approval 
1. All service provider requirements shall be satisfied. 
2. Prior to this Conditional Use Permit/Subdivision/Master Planned Development taking 
effect, the associated Rezone and Master Planned Development (file #21-260) shall be 
approved through the adoption of an ordinance by the Summit County Council. If the 
County Council fails to approve the Rezone and Master Planned Development, this 
Conditional Use Permit/Subdivision/Master Planned Development will be null and void. 
3. Prior to building permit issuance for any structure that has the potential to ridgeline in a 
manner that causes any portion of the structure to extend into the skyline as viewed 
from a public road, Staff shall ensure the structure complies with section 11-2-4-I of the 
Code, as amended.  
4. Minor changes to the Conditional Use Permit may be necessary to ensure compliance 
with Building Code requirements. If such changes will not affect the overall massing, 
scale, character, and/or function of the buildings, they may be approved by the 
Community Development Director or authorized designee. 
5. Signs are not approved as part of this Conditional Use Permit or Subdivision Plat 
approval. Any proposed signs are subject to review and approval of the Community 
Development Department prior to erection. 
6. The Conditional Use Permit shall expire one year from the date of approval unless the 
Conditional Use Permit activity has begun, or an extension is requested in writing by the 
applicant and granted by the Community Development Director. 
7. The applicant shall record a deed restriction, in a form approved by Summit County, to 
preserve a minimum of 312.30 acres of open space, of which a maximum of 156 acres of 
that open space will have less than a 30% slope, concurrent with recordation of the 
subdivision plat.   
8. Prior to construction of any roads associated with the Farms at Blue Sky, a Low Impact 
Permit for any portion of roads that cross 30% slopes shall be reviewed and approved by 
the Community Development Director. 
9. Prior to building permit issuance for any structure, the Summit County Engineering 
Department shall review and approve the construction drawings for the infrastructure. 
111
19 
 
10. The applicant shall ensure that all emergency services personnel have access to the 
property, at all times. 
 
Public Notice, Meetings and Comments:  
This item was publicly noticed as a public hearing with possible action by the ESCPC. Notice of 
this public hearing was published in the November 6, 2024, issue of The Summit County News. 
Postcard courtesy notices were also mailed to property owners within 1,000 feet of the subject 
parcel prior to the public hearings. As of the date of this report, Staff received an inquiry from 
one property owner requesting additional information about the development.

112
Amendment to the Blue-Sky Ranch, LLC 2006 Conditional Use Permit 
The applicant is seeking approval for the Farms at Blue Sky Residential Subdivision ("the 
Farms") through an amendment to the exiting Blue Sky Ranch Conditional Use Permit ("Original 
CUP"). The Original CUP was approved and processed under the use of a Guest Ranch or Lodge 
as defined in the Eastern Summit County Development Code as, "a commercial use consisting 
of recreational activities that may include, but are not limited to, horseback riding, fishing, 
hunting, skiing, and snowmobiling. The guest ranch may include overnight lodging, food 
service, meeting, and conference facilities as well as other uses." The Farms would be the 
residential component of the existing Lodge at Blue Sky comprised of forty-six guest rooms, 
Yuta restaurant, the Edge Spa and pool area. The Farms would also be an extension of the 
Crescent Lodge and the 16 Crescent rooms approved by the Eastern Summit County Planning 
Commission on February 1, 2024, File #22-167. 
The Rezone and Master Panned Development/Subdivision applications for the Farms were 
submitted to the County Planning Department in 2021. The Planning Department 
acknowledged receipt and completeness of the Applications in an email to the Applicant, dated 
January 12, 2022. The Planning Department has indicated that all the previous Applications for 
the Farms are active. The staff recommended revisions to the previously submitted subdivision 
plat to include the amenities for the Farms residential community. The Applicant welcomed the 
staff's recommendation. This recommendation requires that a new "Amendment to Conditional 
Use Permit" application be submitted by the Applicant. 
History 
2004 
Michael Phillips purchased 1007 acres outside of Wanship at the end of Old Lincoln Highway 
with the intent of creating a working Ranch and a hospitality component. He named it Blue Sky 
Ranch. 
2006 Conditional Use Permit History 
2006 
The County issued a Conditional Use Permit to Blue Sky Ranch to operate a corporate retreat on 
the property. The CUP allowed the property to be a corporate university including a retreat 
with a guest lodge that would provide lodging for daily or extended stays, educational and team 
building opportunities, and other attractions and activities intended to serve and attract guests 
and tourists to the property.

EXHIBIT A79
191
Meridian Engineering, Inc. 
1628 West 11010 South, Suite 102  S outh Jordan, Utah 84095 
Phone: 801.569.1315  Fax: 801.569.1319 
Memorandum 
To: Roy Williams 
CC: Blue Sky Contracting 
From: Mark Cook, PE 
Date: 10/10/24 
Subject: Farms at Blue Sky Water Use Summary 
Project No. 19031 
Memo 
The following is a summary of the Water Use demands for the Farms at Blue Sky Subdivision and a summary of the 
measured water supply capacity of the newly drilled will for the project.  
The Blue-Sky Subdivision includes 72 residential lots, an amenities area, consisting of 6 buildings, a future Wastewater 
Treatment Plant and an existing residence (Stillman Residence). Residential water demands for these areas are 
included in the following summary.  
The projected water demand for each residential lot will be 800 gallons per day or one Equivalent Residential Unit 
(ERU).  The Amenities area will require 6,500 gallons per day (8 ERU’s), based on sewer treatment estimates and 
adding in the Stillman Residence and future Water Treatment Plant. The project estimates a total water use demand 
of 64,000 gpd demand (80 ERU’s). This equates to a yearly water demand of 36 acre-ft of water for residential use. 
As part of the project, a new 500,000-gallon storage tank, providing 89 ERU’s of storage, per state standards, along 
with a 3-hour flow at 1,100 gpm for fire flow. This tank will be located at the West end of Rockcress Road.  
The new well needs to provide 800 gpd per EDU for a peak flow of 71,200 gpd (peak flow) or 49.45 gpm.  The well has 
been tested and a steady flow rate of 80 gpm was observed.  The maximum safe yield is 2/3 of the tested yield, resulting 
in a projected flow rate of 53.33 gpm (96 ERU’s). This equates to 43.2 acre-ft of water provided yearly. This flow rate 
exceeds the 80 ERU’s required for the Farms Subdivision.  
The distribution system for the Farms Subdivision includes pipe to all lots and developed area. Due to the change in 
elevation across the site, the site has been divided into 6 pressure zones, with 10 pressure reducing valves.  The 
pressure in each zone will range from 55 to 100 psi during normal operation with the use of these pressure reducing 
valves. During Fire Flow situations (1,000 to 1,100 gpm) the distribution system expects a total flow of 1,250 gpm in 
the system. The anticipated pressures in this scenario are listed in the table below. 
EXHIBIT B1

Subject: Opinion of Water Supply Radiologic Chemicals (Radionuclides) 
Blue Sky Well 3 (WS001), Blue Sky Residential 
Public Water System (PWS) #22155, Summit County, Utah  
for Blue Sky Contracting 
Dear Roy: 
This letter provides my professional opinion of the potential for the presence of 
radionuclides exceeding regulatory limits in water produced from the  Blue Sky Well 3 
(the well) for Blue Sky Residential, Utah Division of Drinking Water (DDW), Public Water 
System (PWS) #22155. Blue Sky Well 3 is DDW Source WS001. 
WATER QUALITY 
Loughlin Water collected a water sample on October 5, 2024, at the end of the 80-gpm 
constant-rate pumping test for analysis by Chemtech of the parameters required by 
Utah Administrative Code (UAC) R309-515-4(5) for a new PWS well for a non-transient, 
community system, which includes an analysis of radionuclides (Gross Alpha, Gross 
Beta, Radium-228, and Radium-226 if the Gross Alpha concentration is greater than 5 
picocuries per liter (pCi/L). The results for radionuclides take about 6 weeks for analysis 
depending on the radiologic activity of the water and Chemtech will not complete th e 
analysis of the radionuclide parameters until the first half of November 2024.  
OPINION OF RADIONUCLIDE LEVELS IN BLUE SKY WELL 3 
We attached the radiologic results from the Blue Sky Replacement Well (Attachment A) 
which show the radiologic activity at concentrations well below EPA  Maximum 
Contaminant Levels  (MCL). Although we cannot guarantee that radiologic activity 
results in the Blue Sky Well 3 are below the MCL for radiochemistry, we believe that the 
results should be similar to those observed in the Blue Sky Replacement well because: 
• The two wells are located relatively close to each other;
• Both wells have excellent water quality based on the analyzed results;

Subject: The Farms at Blue Sky Big Game Wildlife Habitat Assessment 
Approximately 550-Acre Property, Wanship, Summit County, Utah 
De
ar Mr. Phillips: 
P
er your request, Frontier Corporation USA (Frontier) completed a big game wildlife habitat 
assessment for the approximately 550 -acre The Farms at Blue Sky property in Wanship, Summit 
County, Utah (Figure 1).  The property is located directly northwest of the town of Wanship in the 
montane foothills north and west of U.S. Interstate Highway 80 (I-80).  The Property covers most 
of Section 1
8 in Township 1 North, Range 5 East, Salt Lake Base and Meridian (Figure 2a).   
Eleva
tions within the property range from approximately 5, 990 feet to 7,015 feet above mean sea 
level.  Aspect is mostly south and  east facing. The property has two subwatersheds; one draining 
southward toward Silver Creek, and one draining eastward toward the Weber River (Figure 2a).    
W
ater is scarce on the property.  The National Wetlands Inventory does not show any wetlands on 
the property other than the intermittent drainages identified on the U.S. Geological Survey 
(USGS) topographic mapping (Figure 2a).  Previous habitat mapping done by Frontier for the 
Blue Sky Corporate Ranch generally included dry scrub-oak, sagebrush and upland grassland 
plant communities on south and east facing slopes, and patches of coniferous forest on north and 
west facing slopes.   
Review of aerial imagery suggests that these vegetation patterns are similar 
for The Farms at Blue Sky property, although The Farms at Blue Sky property appears to be 
much drier 
(Figure 2b).  
The
 purpose of the assessment is to provide The Farms at Blue Sky information about potential 
big game habitat for elk, mule deer and moose for consideration in planning the future 
development of the property.  The study area for the assessment includes the 550-acre property 
and the lands adjacent to the property.   
Ha
bitat mapping data for elk, mule deer and moose were obtained from the Utah Division of 
Wildlife Resources (UDWR) via the Utah Automated Geographic Reference Center (AGRC) web 
link.  Frontier downloaded the habitat mapping data and incorporated it into a GIS data base.  The 
GIS was used to overlay the habitat mapping with USGS topographic base maps (Figures 3, 4 and 
5).   
The
 UDWR habitat mapping has two components: 
1) Habitat value (Crucial or Substantial), and
2) S
easonal use (Winter, Spring, Summer, Fall and/or Year-long).
Environmental Consultants 
EXHIBIT I1
228
Mr. Michael C. Phillips 
The Farms at Blue Sky 
January 29, 2021 
Page 2 
of 2 
 
 
The UDWR definitions for the two habitat components area as follows: 
 
Crucial value - habitat on which the local population of a wildlife species depends for survival 
because there are no alternative ranges or habitats available.  Crucial value habitat is essential to 
the life history requirements of a wildlife species.  Degradation or unavailability of crucial habitat 
will lead to significant declines in carrying capacity and/or numbers of wildlife species in 
question. 
 
Substantial value - 
habitat used by a wildlife species but is not crucial for population survival. 
Degradation or unavailability of substantial value habitat will not lead to significant declines in 
carrying capacity and/or numbers of the wildlife species in question. 
 
Based upon the UDWR habitat mapping data, the study area contains crucial summer, fall and 
winter habitat for elk, substantial winter habitat for mule deer, but no crucial or
 substantial habitat 
for moose.   
 
Elk 
Elk habitat has been designated by the UDWR throughout the entirety of the property and on 
lands immediately adjacent to the property (Figure 3).  Approximately 42 acres of the highest 
elevation land in the northwestern corner of the property is designated as crucial summer/fall 
habitat.  The remainder of the property is designated as crucial
 winter habitat.   The habitat 
mapping shows significant amounts of crucial summer/fall elk habitat extending north and west of 
the property.  Crucial winter elk habitat is 
shown extending north, south and east of the property. 
 
Mule Deer 
Mule deer habitat has been designated by the UDWR throughout the entirety of the p roperty and 
on lands adjacent to the property (Figure 4).  The entirety of the mule deer habitat mapping for the 
property and surrounding lands is classified as substantial winter habitat. 
 
Moose 
There are no habitat designations for moose covering the property or on the lands adjacent to the 
property (Figure 5).
  Crucial year-long habitat is shown approximately 2 miles west of the 
property.  Crucial winter habitat is shown approximately 1.0  mile east of the property on the east 
side of I-80 and the Weber River. 
 
Please feel free to call me if you have any questions about the big game wildlife habitat 
assessment 
for the Farms at Blue Sky Property. 
     
Sincerely, 
Frontier Corporation USA 
 
Dennis C. Wenger 
Senior 
Ecologist/Principal

EXHIBIT K1

Wanship - The Farms at Blue Sky  
Traffic Impact Study 
 
 
 
 
 i
  
EXECUTIVE SUMMARY 
This study addresses the traffic impacts associated with the proposed The Farms at Blue Sky 
d
evelopment located in Wanship, Utah. The development is located on the north side of Old 
Lincoln Highway between Blue Sky Road and West Hoytsville Road. 
The purpose of this traffic impact study is to analyze traffic operations at key intersections for 
existing (2024) and future (2029) conditions with and without the proposed project and to 
recommend mitigation measures as needed. The morning and evening peak hour level of service 
(LOS) results are shown in Table ES-1. A site plan of the project is provided in Appendix A. 
Table ES-1: Peak Hour Level of Service Results 
 
SUMMARY OF KEY FINDINGS & RECOMMENDATIONS 
Project Conditions 
• The development will consist of 72 residential lots 
• The project is anticipated to generate approximately 634 weekday daily trips, including 48 trips in the 
morning peak hour, and 63 trips in the evening peak hour 
• Based on the anticipated project traffic, no auxiliary lanes are recommended 
2024 Background Plus Project 
Findings • Acceptable LOS • Acceptable LOS 
Mitigations • None • None 
2029 Background Plus Project 
Assumptions • Anticipated traffic from Hoytsville 
development accounted for • None 
Findings • Acceptable LOS • Acceptable LOS 
Mitigations • None • None

EXHIBIT K4
242
Wanship - The Farms at Blue Sky  
Traffic Impact Study 
 
 
 
 
 1
  
I.  INTRODUCTION 
A. Purpose 
This study addresses the traffic impacts associated with the proposed The Farms at Blue Sky 
d
evelopment located in Wanship, Utah. The proposed project is located on the north side of Old 
Lincoln Highway between Blue Sky Road and West Hoytsville Road. Figure 1 shows a vicinity 
map of the proposed development. 
The purpose of this traffic impact study is to analyze traffic operations at key intersections for 
existing (2024) and future (2029) conditions with and without the proposed project and to 
recommend mitigation measures as needed. 
 
Figure 1: Vicinity map showing the project location in Wanship, Utah 
EXHIBIT K5
243
Wanship - The Farms at Blue Sky  
Traffic Impact Study 
 
 
 
 
 2
  
B. Scope 
The study area was defined based on conversations with the development team. This study was 
s
coped to evaluate the traffic operational performance impacts of the project on the following 
intersections: 
• Blue Sky Drive / Old Lincoln Highway 
• Barn Property Access / Old Lincoln Highway 
• SR-32 / Old Lincoln highway 
C. Analysis Methodology 
Level of service (LOS) is a term that describes the operating performance of an intersection or 
r
oadway. LOS is measured quantitatively and reported on a scale from A to F, with A representing 
the best performance and F the worst. Table 1 provides a brief description of each LOS letter 
designation and an accompanying average delay per vehicle for both signalized and unsignalized 
intersections. 
The Highway Capacity Manual (HCM), 7 th Edition, 2022 methodology was used in this study to 
r
emain consistent with “state-of-the-practice” professional standards. This methodology has 
different quantitative evaluations for signalized and unsignalized intersections. For signalized, 
roundabout, and all-way stop-controlled (AWSC) intersections, the LOS is provided for the overall 
intersection (weighted average of all approach delays). For all other unsignalized intersections, 
LOS is reported based on the worst movement. 
Using Synchro/SimTraffic software, which follow the HCM methodology, the peak hour LOS was 
computed for each study intersection. Multiple runs of SimTraffic were used to provide a statistical 
evaluation of the interaction between the intersections. The detailed LOS reports are provided in 
Appendix C. Hales Engineering also calculated the 95 th percentile queue lengths for the study 
i
ntersections using SimTraffic. The detailed queue length reports are provided in Appendix D. 
Many of the figures in this report are printouts of the Synchro model. These figures are not meant 
to be a design exhibit for exact lane striping and design, due to the limitations of the Synchro 
software. Instead, the purpose of these figures is to show assumed peak hour turning movement 
volumes and the conceptual travel lane configuration of the study roadway network. 
D. Level of Service Standards 
For the purposes of this study, a minimum acceptable intersection performance for each of the 
s
tudy intersections was set at LOS D. If levels of service E or F conditions exist, an explanation 
and/or mitigation measures will be presented. A LOS D threshold is consistent with “state-of-the-
practice” traffic engineering principles for urbanized areas.

 4
  
II.  EXISTING (2024) BACKGROUND CONDITIONS 
A. Purpose 
The purpose of the background analysis is to study the intersections and roadways during the 
p
eak travel periods of the day with background traffic and geometric conditions. Through this 
analysis, background traffic operational deficiencies can be identified, and potential mitigation 
measures recommended. This analysis provides a baseline condition that may be compared to 
the build conditions to identify the impacts of the development. 
B. Roadway System 
The primary roadways that will provide access to the project site are described below: 
O
ld Lincoln Highway – is a county-maintained roadway which is classified by the Eastern Summit 
County Transportation Master Plan as a collector. The roadway has one travel lane in each 
direction. The posted speed limit is 25 mph in the study area. 
C. Traffic Volumes 
Weekday morning (8:00 to 10:00 a.m.) and evening (3:30 to 5:30 p.m.) peak period traffic counts 
w
ere performed at the following intersections: 
• Blue Sky Drive / Old Lincoln Highway 
• Barn Property Access / Old Lincoln Highway 
• SR-32 / Old Lincoln highway 
The counts were performed on Wednesday, July 17, 2024. The morning peak hour was 
determined to be between 8:45 and 9:45 a.m., and the evening peak hour was determined to be 
between 4:00 and 5:00 p.m. The evening peak hour volumes were approximately 34% higher 
than the morning peak hour volumes. Both the morning and evening peak hour volumes were 
used in the analysis. Detailed count data are included in Appendix B. 
Hales Engineering considered making seasonal adjustments to the observed traffic volumes. 
Monthly traffic volume data were obtained from a nearby UDOT automatic traffic recorder (ATR) 
on I-80 (ATR #318). In recent years, traffic volumes in July have been higher than average traffic 
volumes. As a conservative measure, the observed traffic volumes were not adjusted at the study 
intersections. 
Figure 2 shows the existing morning and evening peak hour volumes as well as intersection 
geometry at the study intersections.

 8
  
III.  PROJECT CONDITIONS 
A. Purpose 
The project conditions discussion explains the type and intensity of development. This provides 
t
he basis for trip generation, distribution, and assignment of project trips to the surrounding study 
intersections defined in Chapter I.  
B. Project Description 
The proposed The Farms at Blue Sky development is located on the north side of Old Lincoln 
H
ighway between Blue Sky Road and West Hoytsville Road. The development will consist of 72 
lots for single family homes. A concept plan for the proposed development is provided in Appendix 
A. 
C. Multi Modal Analysis 
Since the project is located in a rural area, there are no transit stops nearby. It is not anticipated 
t
hat there will be a demand for any form of transit in the area in the foreseeable future. Currently, 
there are no sidewalks or multi-use paths along Old Lincoln Highway. According to Utah’s Unified 
Transportation plan, there are plans for a multi-use paths to be installed along Old Lincoln 
Highway and SR-32 as phase 3 projects. 
D. Trip Generation 
Trip generation for the development was calculated using trip generation rates published in the 
I
nstitute of Transportation Engineers (ITE), Trip Generation , 11 th Edition, 2021. Some trip 
r
eductions were made to account for primary/secondary residences. Based on data collected at 
similar mountain communities in Summit County, it is estimated that residences will be no more 
than 85% occupied. To remain conservative in our analysis, an 85% maximum occupancy was 
assumed for the development. Trip generation for the proposed project is included in Table 3. 
The total trip generation for the development is as follows: 
• Daily Trips:      634 
• Morning Peak Hour Trips:     48 
• Evening Peak Hour Trips:     63 
EXHIBIT K12
250
Wanship - The Farms at Blue Sky  
Traffic Impact Study

Table 3: Trip Generation 
 
E. Trip Distribution and Assignment 
Project traffic is assigned to the roadway network based on the type of trip and the proximity of 
p
roject access points to major streets, high population densities, and regional trip attractions. 
Existing travel patterns observed during data collection also provide helpful guidance to 
establishing these distribution percentages, especially near the site. The resulting distribution of 
project generated trips during the morning and evening peak hour is shown in Table 4. 
Table 4: Trip Distribution 
Direction % To/From Project 
South 85% 
East 15% 
These trip distribution assumptions were used to assign the morning and evening peak hour trip 
generation at the study intersections to create trip assignment for the proposed development. Trip 
assignment for the development is shown in Figure 3. 
F. Access 
The proposed access for the site will be gained at the following location: 
O
ld Lincoln Highway: 
• The project access will be located approximately 100 feet west of the Barn Property 
Access / Old Lincoln Highway intersection where the dirt road is currently located. It 
will access the project on the north side of Road A. It is anticipated that the access will 
be stop-controlled. The Barn Property Access will be replaced with this road.

 12
  
G. Auxiliary Lanes 
Auxiliary lanes are deceleration (ingress) or acceleration (egress) turn lanes that provide for safe 
t
urning movements that have less impact on through traffic. These lanes are sometimes needed 
at accesses or roadway intersections if right- or left-turn volumes are high enough.  
Deceleration (ingress) lanes are generally needed when there are at least 50 right-turn vehicles 
or 25 left-turn vehicles in an hour. These guidelines were used for the City roadways in the study 
area. Based on these guidelines and the anticipated project traffic, no auxiliary lanes are 
recommended.

IV.  EXISTING (2024) PLUS PROJECT CONDITIONS 
A. Purpose 
The purpose of the existing (2024) plus project analysis is to study the intersections and roadways 
d
uring the peak travel periods of the day for existing background traffic and geometric conditions 
plus the net trips generated by the proposed development. This scenario provides valuable insight 
into the potential impacts of the proposed project on background traffic conditions. 
B. Traffic Volumes 
Hales Engineering added the project trips discussed in Chapter III to the existing (2024) 
b
ackground traffic volumes to predict turning movement volumes for existing (2024) plus project 
conditions. Existing (2024) plus project morning and evening peak hour turning movement 
volumes are shown in Figure 4. 
C. Level of Service Analysis 
Hales Engineering determined that all intersections are anticipated to operate at acceptable levels 
o
f service during the morning and evening peak hours with project traffic added, as shown in 
Table 5.

 16
  
V.  FUTURE (2029) BACKGROUND CONDITIONS 
A. Purpose 
The purpose of the future (2029) background analysis is to study the intersections and roadways 
d
uring the peak travel periods of the day for future background traffic and geometric conditions. 
Through this analysis, future background traffic operational deficiencies can be identified, and 
potential mitigation measures recommended. 
B. Roadway Network 
As mentioned previously, Utah’s Unified Transportation Plan shows that trails are planned to be 
c
onstructed along Old Lincoln Highway and SR-32. These trails are listed as a phase 3 project 
and are not planned before 2029 in the study area. No other projects are listed in the study area, 
therefore, no changes were made to the roadway network for the future (2029) analysis. 
C. Traffic Volumes 
Hales Engineering obtained future (2029) forecasted volumes from the Summit / Wasatch County 
t
ravel demand model. Peak period turning movement counts were estimated using National 
Cooperative Highway Research Program (NCHRP) 255 methodologies which utilize existing peak 
period turn volumes and future average weekday daily traffic (AWDT) volumes to project the 
future turn volumes at the major intersections. Future (2029) morning and evening peak hour 
turning movement volumes are shown in Figure 5. 
Anticipated 2030 traffic volumes from a large development currently being planned in Hoytsville 
were accounted for and added to the future (2029) background conditions as a conservative 
measure. 
D. Level of Service Analysis 
Hales Engineering determined that all study intersections are anticipated to operate at acceptable 
l
evels of service during the morning and evening peak hours in future (2029) background 
conditions, as shown in Table 6. These results serve as a baseline condition for the impact 
analysis of the proposed development for future (2029) conditions. 
E. Queuing Analysis 
Hales Engineering calculated the 95th percentile queue lengths for each of the study intersections. 
N
o significant queueing is anticipated during the morning and evening peak hours.  
F. Mitigation Measures 
No mitigation measures are recommended.
